## October 13, 2026 — Test 20 Results and Next CRM Work

### Verified / deferred testing
- Rob completed Test 20 work with IT present. The controlled Microsoft Graph smoke test passed, and the single test email send passed with no issue. Treat Graph SEND as verified for the tested path.
- Do not mark mailbox READ as verified; a read test has not been performed.
- Tests 3 and 4 were intentionally NOT RUN / DEFERRED, not failed. They would send a larger batch of real customer emails outside the normal monthly cycle. Rob plans to exercise the larger live-send path during the next normal monthly send, expected in roughly one week.
- IT indicated send permission may already have existed and simply had not been confirmed. The October 13 successful send is the authoritative evidence that sending currently works.

### CRM Graph access
- GoGreen Monthly Drafts currently has the working Graph email path. Rob is speaking with IT about ensuring the CRM portion also has the required Microsoft Graph/mail access so CRM-originated workflows can send email.
- Do not assume CRM Graph access is complete until Rob/IT confirms it and CRM itself is tested.

### Next implementation: Box Order emails
- After the Excel box order form is generated, reviewed, and approved, support two separate CRM-originated emails.
- Email 1: Order Processing. Send the approved order form as an attachment and request that Order Processing process the request.
- Email 2: Customer acknowledgment. Tell the customer that the box request/order has been submitted/approved using the approved standard wording.
- Preserve the existing approval gate. Approval of the order must complete before either email can be sent; email automation must never approve the underlying order.
- Both emails use the same optional review/edit behavior. Provide a checkbox such as `Review/Edit Email Before Sending`. Unchecked: use the approved standard template without opening the extra editor. Checked: open a small editor populated with the complete generated email, allow the user to add special instructions or modify wording, then explicitly Send or Cancel. Cancel must send nothing.
- Keep Order Processing and customer messages as separate sends; do not combine them into one message.
- Reuse the existing configurable Order Processing recipient settings where applicable rather than hard-coding addresses.

### Explicitly deferred / out of scope
- Do not implement FedEx/UPS shipping-label email automation as part of this task. Rob wants FedEx and UPS API integration working before that workflow is designed/automated.
- Do not expand this work into carrier-label generation or other unrelated email automation.

### Application naming decision
- Rob has decided to rename the entire Weekly Logger Search Application project/application to **CRM**, rather than using CRM only as a form/user-facing label.
- Treat the application-wide rename as a separate implementation task from Graph/Box Order email work. Before changing names, inventory the solution/project/executable/root namespace/settings/deployment/test/documentation references so the rename is complete and does not break persisted settings, database paths, packaging, tests, or deployment behavior.

### Codex execution guidance
- Before implementation, read this handoff and inspect the current code paths for order approval, order-form generation/attachment, Graph sending, recipient settings, existing email templates, project/solution naming, packaging, and deployment. Preserve current business rules unless this section explicitly changes them.
- **Execution order:** (1) establish and verify the merge checkpoint above; (2) inventory the existing implementation and identify exactly what can be reused; (3) implement/test the CRM Graph/Box Order email work that does not require unconfirmed live authorization; (4) if CRM live-send authorization is still unresolved, stop at that live-test boundary and report the blocker rather than guessing or weakening safeguards; (5) perform the application-wide rename to **CRM** as its own separately verifiable change set, not intermingled with the email implementation.
- Keep the implementation lean: use the existing Graph/email infrastructure where practical rather than introducing a parallel mail subsystem. Do not duplicate authentication, configuration, recipient, template, or send logic unless the current architecture genuinely requires separation.
- For the two Box Order emails, preserve the existing approval transaction as the authoritative gate. Both sends occur only after approval has successfully committed. Order Processing receives the approved Excel order form attachment; the customer acknowledgment is a separate message and must not accidentally inherit the internal attachment unless an existing approved business rule explicitly requires it.
- Resolve recipients from existing CRM data/settings and current business rules; do not invent or hard-code customer or Order Processing addresses. If the current code does not provide an authoritative customer-recipient rule, document that as a blocker/decision needed instead of guessing.
- The review/edit checkbox applies independently to the generated email being sent. When enabled, the editor must display the complete generated message before send and provide explicit Send and Cancel actions. Cancel sends nothing and must not undo the already committed order approval.
- A failure of either email send must be reported/logged clearly, must not roll back the committed order approval, and must not falsely mark the failed message as sent. Keep the two send outcomes distinguishable so one successful message does not mask failure of the other.
- Add regression tests for the approval gate, automatic-template path, review/edit path, Cancel-without-send, Order Processing attachment handling, customer message separation, recipient resolution, separate send outcomes, and send failures. Preserve existing relevant tests and business rules.
- For the rename, first inventory solution name, project name, assembly/executable identity, root namespace, user-facing application name, settings/configuration paths, database paths, deployment/package scripts, tests, documentation, shortcuts/launch assumptions, and any persisted identifiers. Preserve backward compatibility for existing user data/settings where needed; do not silently strand or overwrite existing CRM data/configuration merely to complete the rename.
- Before reporting completion, run the relevant build and automated regression suites, record exact pass/fail results, and identify any manual/live tests that remain pending. Do not claim mailbox read support, batch-send completion, CRM Graph authorization, carrier API integration, live CRM email sending, or the application rename as complete until each applicable item is separately implemented/tested and confirmed.
- After the task, update this shared handoff with what changed, files/areas changed, branch and commit identifiers, build/test results, manual tests still required, blockers/risks, and whether the work is merged or awaiting review; commit/push and remotely verify that handoff update.

## CRM database recovery read-only investigation findings (October 19, 2026)

### Scope, inputs, and stopping condition

- This was an investigation only. No recovery, merge, application execution, application/source/schema/settings change, production access, live email, test run, package, or deployment was performed. The original installations were not opened. The only intended repository modification is this sanitized handoff report.
- The mounted inputs were verified at `Z:\CRM_Recovery\Backup\Data\CRM.sqlite` and `Z:\CRM_Recovery\Everyday\Data\CRM.sqlite`. Each main file is 2,195,456 bytes. Backup SHA-256 is `5A39A8E77372758DE417392A45B162BB94AD4DAC28721837578913F10E9C3EE6`; Everyday SHA-256 is `308F7718F5FD8D3B4D5AA64B2AB02B5D3F374D8D3DE3787A2576397B90C54140`. The distinct hashes confirm distinct database states.
- At initial enumeration, each copied Data folder contained only `CRM.sqlite`; no WAL, SHM, rollback journal, settings file, or source-connection configuration was present. Consequently the mounted database paths are verified, but the original installations' connection settings cannot be independently verified from these Data-only copies.
- Main-file copied creation times were `2026-10-20T02:33:05.3557769Z` (Backup) and `2026-10-20T02:34:50.9115725Z` (Everyday). Preserved main-file last-write times were `2026-10-19T16:04:56Z` and `2026-10-16T19:57:04Z`, respectively. Backup's latest internal write evidence ends at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1783863Z`; Everyday's ends at `2026-10-16T19:56:12.6310588Z`. These facts are consistent with Rob's statement that work stopped after discovery, but timestamps alone cannot prove that no unlogged or timestamp-preserving access occurred.
- **Mandatory stop / access-method incident:** Microsoft.Data.Sqlite connections were created with `SqliteOpenMode.ReadOnly`, private cache, pooling disabled, and `PRAGMA query_only=ON` (verified as `1`). Both databases report persistent `journal_mode=wal`. Despite the read-only/query-only connection, SQLite created a 32,768-byte `CRM.sqlite-shm` and a zero-byte `CRM.sqlite-wal` in each copied folder during inspection. Backup sidecars were created at `2026-10-20T03:07:42.5476605Z` (SHM) and `2026-10-20T02:59:38.9473094Z` (WAL); Everyday sidecars at `2026-10-20T03:07:42.9656662Z` and `2026-10-20T02:59:59.1335391Z`. Both SHM files hash to `FD4C9FDA9CD3F9AE7C962B0DDF37232294D55580E1AA165AA06129B8549389EB`; both empty WAL files have the standard empty SHA-256 `E3B0C44298FC1C149AFBF4C8996FB92427AE41E4649B934CA495991B7852B855`. The two main database hashes remained unchanged after every query. No further SQLite access was performed once the sidecars were discovered, and the sidecars were not deleted or altered. This means the investigation is evidence-rich but stopped short of any additional checks because companion-file writes could no longer be excluded.

### Read-only method and validation completed before the stop

- Filesystem identity was checked with PowerShell `Get-Item`, `Get-ChildItem`, and `Get-FileHash`. SQLite metadata and rows were read with the repository's Microsoft.Data.Sqlite runtime using explicit read-only connections and `PRAGMA query_only=ON`; comparisons were performed in process between two separately read-only connections. An attempted URI `ATTACH ... mode=ro` failed with SQLite error 14 and attached nothing; main-file hashes remained unchanged. No SQL write statement was issued.
- Both main files returned `PRAGMA integrity_check = ok`, zero `foreign_key_check` rows, `PRAGMA user_version=24`, and `SchemaVersion=24`. Both use 4,096-byte pages and 536 pages. Backup had 12 free pages; Everyday had 14. Their table definitions and schema objects matched; no schema migration is involved.
- The approved source behavior was checked without checkout or modification against remote branch `codex/test22` at `97ed38f311a26f8bd5282cebf13b73d623629982`. The working checkout's isolated unauthorized authentication branch was not run or used as the recovery baseline. Source inspection confirms that `Activity` is the principal record-history/audit surface, normal site/contact saves can update timestamps even when semantic values do not change, contact methods have no timestamps, and a site deletion records one `SiteRemoved` event while soft-deleting related links and otherwise-unshared contacts.
- Whole-table, primary-key-aware comparison found differences only in `Activity`, `Company`, `Site`, `Contact`, `ContactMethod`, `SiteContact`, `RecycleProgramProfile`, and `SiteLocation`. Agreements, agreement items/sites/documents, accounts, aliases, sales/source mappings, imports/issues/rows, items, monthly follow-ups, security tables, associates, users/roles/permissions, and vendor accounts were unchanged. `SecurityAuditEvent` and `SecurityAuditNetworkAddress` are empty in both copies; there is no separate general row-version table.

### Observed October 19 Backup work

- Backup contains 16 history rows created from `2026-10-19T14:23:04.7846715Z` through `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1783863Z`: five `SiteUpdated`, three `ContactAdded`, four `ContactUpdated`, two `BoxOrder`, one `PostersSent`, and one `SiteRemoved`. The Posters event was created October 19 but deliberately records an occurrence date of April 24, 2026; both dates must remain distinct in any future recovery.
- The 16 events affect existing companies C-2 and C-76; existing sites S-3, S-230, S-233, and S-237; existing contacts C-3, C-206, C-220, and C-221; and three new Backup contacts C-507 through C-509. Numeric labels here are sanitized internal IDs, not customer names or personal data.
- Three contacts were added: C-507 at S-237 and C-508/C-509 at S-230. Together they have six new contact-method rows and three new active site-contact links. Each new contact has exactly one site link and two active methods. C-507 also has a subsequent same-day Office phone and Notes update.
- Cleanly evidenced existing-record edits are: S-3 Notes cleared; C-220 and C-221 Notes changed; S-230 Notes changed twice and ended blank; S-237 Notes changed twice and ended at a different value from Everyday; two BoxOrder activities at S-237 linked to C-221 and C-220; `LastInteractionDate` at those two links advanced to October 19; and one backdated PostersSent activity at S-230. Audit old-value fingerprints for S-3, S-230, C-220, and C-221 match Everyday's current values, so these edits begin from the authoritative current value rather than an older divergent value.
- S-233 was soft-deleted. One archive-batch token ties S-233, its two site-contact links, and the two otherwise-unshared contacts C-210/C-211. Backup has those five rows inactive; Everyday has them active. The single `SiteRemoved` history event is company-linked but has null `SiteId` and `ContactId`; its details identify the site, and the batch token/timestamps corroborate S-233, but the foreign-key linkage is incomplete. No separate ContactRemoved events were created for the cascade.
- Backup writes also touched two Company timestamps, four Site timestamps, 11 Contact timestamps, three program-profile timestamps, and three location timestamps. Most of that is timestamp-only save churn and must not overwrite Everyday timestamps. Four existing contact-method rows differ only because Backup marks the sole active email method primary where Everyday marks it non-primary; contact methods have no timestamps, and only one related user-visible phone change has an audit event, so attribution of all four primary-flag differences to October 19 is an inference rather than a proven fact.

### Everyday changes, collisions, and conflicts

- Activity rows 1 through 1346 are identical in both databases. Everyday then added four October 16 events at IDs 1347-1350 (two S-237 updates, one S-237/C-220 BoxOrder, and one unrelated shipping-label event). Backup independently reused IDs 1347-1350 on October 19 and added IDs 1351-1362. Therefore four history primary keys collide with different content; the remaining 12 Backup event IDs are merely unused in the current Everyday snapshot. No future recovery may preserve or copy any Backup `ActivityId`; all 16 must receive new IDs with an explicit old-to-new map.
- Everyday's October 16 S-237 work changed Alias and Notes and added a BoxOrder/interaction for C-220. Backup's October 19 S-237 work began from the older Backup Notes value, not Everyday's then-current Notes, and changed Notes twice. This is a real divergent same-field conflict. Everyday's Alias and October 16 history/interaction must be preserved; Rob must choose or confirm the intended final Notes result before any future recovery applies that field.
- Everyday has an October 16 shipping-label event and `LastInteractionDate` for unrelated S-145/C-141 that Backup lacks because it is older. Those Everyday records must remain untouched.
- C-206 has a material history/state inconsistency: October 19 audit says Mobile phone changed from a value to blank, but Backup still contains that same active phone value. Source review explains that clearing a primary method only removes its primary flag and does not deactivate the method, after which the read path can still return it. The intended recovery result cannot be inferred: Rob must decide whether the phone was intended to be removed. Never treat the audit row alone or current row alone as conclusive.
- C-3 has a JobTitle difference and an October 19 `UpdatedUtc`, but no corresponding ContactUpdated event. S-3's address fingerprint is cleared in Backup while its address columns equal Everyday and the only event records a Notes change. These are incompletely logged differences and require explicit review; they must not be silently copied.
- There are no current primary-key collisions for the three new contacts (Everyday max 506 versus Backup max 509) or six new contact methods (Everyday max 1057 versus Backup max 1063), but future writes could consume those IDs. Safe recovery must still allocate new IDs and map every dependent row. Existing company/site/contact foreign keys are valid in both files, and both databases have zero foreign-key violations.

### Evidence gaps and classification

- Observed fact: both copied main databases are internally valid schema-24 snapshots, and the main-file hashes never changed during investigation. Observed fact: the inspection created WAL/SHM companions despite read-only/query-only settings; this is why SQLite access stopped.
- Observed fact: Backup's latest pre-October-19 history is October 14, while Everyday contains four later October 16 events. Inference: the copied Backup operational baseline is approximately two days behind Everyday for logged activity, not a full week; filesystem copy and last-write times do not establish when the older installation was originally made.
- The Activity audit is strong for the 16 October 19 operations but incomplete for cascaded child deletions, timestamp-only writes, contact-method primary flags, C-3 JobTitle, S-3 fingerprint clearing, and direct linkage of the SiteRemoved event. No database-wide immutable revision log exists to resolve those gaps automatically.
- Customer names, contact names, addresses, emails, phone numbers, note contents, order-form paths, and raw database rows are intentionally omitted from this GitHub handoff. The secure evidence remains only in the mounted local database copies and can be re-derived after Rob authorizes a safer immutable inspection method.

4. Insert the three new contacts and six methods with newly allocated IDs, maintain an old-to-new contact map, then add the three site links. Do not copy primary keys or whole rows.
5. Apply approved existing-record field changes with optimistic preconditions against the reviewed Everyday values. Recreate the two October 19 LastInteractionDate updates only after verifying they do not regress a newer current date.
6. Reproduce the S-233 soft-delete transactionally using a deliberate archive-batch mapping, including its two links and two otherwise-unshared contacts, only after Rob confirms the deletion. Preserve restore semantics and add/recreate properly linked history rather than blindly copying the original history row.
7. Recreate all 16 October 19 events with new Activity IDs while preserving original `ActivityUtc`, `CreatedUtc`, activity type, associate, summary/details, and mapped relationships. Preserve the backdated Posters occurrence and resolve the SiteRemoved linkage explicitly. Never overwrite Everyday IDs 1347-1350 or copy history rows without remapping.
8. On the disposable result, run integrity/foreign-key checks, primary-key and relationship audits, targeted before/after value checks, history chronology review, active/inactive counts, and application-level manual acceptance under separate authorization. Produce a rollback-ready evidence package before any production replacement is considered.

### Explicit non-actions and current disposition

- No application code, repository-local CRM handoff, settings, schema, database row, production file, original installation, package, deployment, email, or branch was changed. No builds, tests, CRM executable, migrations, Graph operations, or live workflows were run.

## CRM recovery conflict comparison findings (October 19, 2026)

**Status: completed after commit/push/remote-hash verification.** This was the authorized comparison only. No recovery, merge, database write, CRM execution, application/source/schema/settings change, test, email, package, or deployment was performed.

### Copy safety and method

- Before any SQLite access, all three files in each source Data folder were copied to a unique disposable local directory. Each source file was opened through a .NET `FileStream` with `FileAccess.Read` and `FileShare.Read`; SQLite was never pointed at either `Z:` source. All six destination hashes matched their sources before inspection. The zero-byte WAL files establish that these particular copied companions contain no uncheckpointed frames; the SHM files were preserved with their associated main files.
- SQLite access was limited to the disposable copies, using `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite` with `Mode=ReadOnly`, private cache, pooling disabled, and `PRAGMA query_only=ON`. The working main-file hashes remained `5A39A8E77372758DE417392A45B162BB94AD4DAC28721837578913F10E9C3EE6` (Backup) and `308F7718F5FD8D3B4D5AA64B2AB02B5D3F374D8D3DE3787A2576397B90C54140` (Everyday). Working WAL files remained empty. SQLite advanced only the disposable SHM last-write timestamps; both working SHM contents still hashed to `FD4C9FDA9CD3F9AE7C962B0DDF37232294D55580E1AA165AA06129B8549389EB`. This writable-copy effect did not reach the evidence sources.
- Final source inventory and hashes exactly matched the pre-copy inventory: both main-file hashes above; both 32,768-byte SHM files at `FD4C9FDA9CD3F9AE7C962B0DDF37232294D55580E1AA165AA06129B8549389EB`; and both zero-byte WAL files at `E3B0C44298FC1C149AFBF4C8996FB92427AE41E4649B934CA495991B7852B855`. Source sizes, creation times, and last-write times were also unchanged. No source companion was deleted, cleaned up, or modified.
- Exact private contact values remain excluded from GitHub. The complete S-237 Notes values requested by Rob are included below because they contain operational context but no contact address, email, phone number, or raw database row. Other sensitive values are described by type/state only.

### S-237 Notes — first-priority conflict

- **Current authoritative Everyday value:** SQL `NULL` (no Notes text), with profile `UpdatedUtc=2026-10-16T19:32:45.6458802+00:00`. **Current Backup value:** `Windigo Logistics sending in for Kroger.`, with profile `UpdatedUtc=2026-10-19T15:12:43.7915307+00:00`.
- The complete older text from which both sequences began was `box order\n\nWindigo Logistics sending in for Kroger.  Ben is trying to findout how many are collected evry month. Email inquiry` (the first audit in each database also records a CRLF-to-LF normalization of the same content).
- Everyday Activity 1347 at `2026-10-16T19:32:18.5073879+00:00` records the newline normalization while also changing Alias from `38` to `King Soopers`; Activity 1348 at `2026-10-16T19:32:45.6459500+00:00` records the full Notes value changing to empty. Thus Everyday intentionally cleared the field on October 16.
- Backup Activity 1348 at `2026-10-19T14:35:33.5300145+00:00` records the same newline normalization from its stale pre-October-16 value; Activity 1350 at `2026-10-19T14:35:56.7608122+00:00` records that full value changing to `Windigo Logistics sending in for Kroger.` Thus, relative to the older Backup value, October 19 replaced the multi-part note with one retained sentence and removed `box order` plus the sentence beginning `Ben is trying`; relative to current Everyday, it adds the retained sentence to an empty field.
- The later Backup profile timestamp does not prove an additional Notes edit because no later Notes audit exists. The two branches contain genuinely divergent user actions; neither timestamp alone resolves intent.
- **Resolution options, none implemented:** (A) retain Everyday `NULL` and preserve the October 19 sentence only in recreated audit/history; (B) set Notes to the Backup sentence while preserving both October 16 clearing history and October 19 history; (C) place the Backup sentence in a separately chosen current note/history location while leaving this field empty. Do not restore the full older text unless Rob explicitly says the removed `box order` and `Ben is trying` content should return. **Question for Rob:** after seeing the October 16 clear and October 19 reduction side by side, what should S-237's current Notes field contain?

### Other decision points

- **C-206 MobilePhone:** Everyday and Backup both currently contain one active MobilePhone method with the same email-formatted value; it is not a conventional phone number. Backup Activity 1357 at `2026-10-19T15:58:04.3467516+00:00` says that value changed to blank, yet the row remains active and unchanged. C-206 `UpdatedUtc` is `2026-08-23T20:48:26.7737446+00:00` in Everyday and `2026-10-19T15:59:00.7800026+00:00` in Backup. Contact methods have no per-row timestamp. Verified fact: audit and resulting Backup row disagree. Source behavior previously reviewed can explain this as a clear action that removed primary selection without deactivating the method, but user intent remains unknown. **Options:** keep the row, deactivate/remove it in a future authorized recovery, or separately correct its method type only if Rob confirms that was intended. **Question:** was October 19 intended to remove this malformed MobilePhone value completely?
- **C-3 JobTitle:** Everyday stores an empty string; Backup stores SQL `NULL`. Backup C-3 `UpdatedUtc=2026-10-19T14:23:04.7858119+00:00`; Everyday retains `2026-08-23T20:48:26.4936594+00:00`. No ContactUpdated event records a title edit. Both values are user-visible blank, so this may be save-time normalization rather than a meaningful edit, but that is interpretation. **Options:** preserve Everyday's empty string or normalize to `NULL`; neither preserves additional title text because none exists. **Question:** should blank titles be normalized to `NULL`, or should this unaudited difference be excluded?
- **Four Email primary flags:** ContactMethod IDs 5/C-3, 436/C-205, 438/C-206, and 441/C-207 are active and valid in both databases. Everyday has `IsPrimary=0`; Backup has `IsPrimary=1`. Each is the contact's sole active Email row. No Activity entry or method timestamp attributes these changes to October 19. **Options:** preserve Everyday flags, adopt Backup flags, or apply a separately approved invariant making each sole active Email primary. **Question:** were these deliberate primary selections, or incidental normalization during the October 19 saves?
- **S-3 address fingerprint:** Address columns are identical in both copies. Everyday has a 50-character normalized-address fingerprint (SHA-256 of the exact fingerprint text `04B542B21F67FAFDC475F6550DEAA491C51A64EA8704FCA7F94426ABB8B42D95`) and `SiteLocation.UpdatedUtc=2026-08-26T09:52:47.3974017+00:00`; Backup has `NULL` and `UpdatedUtc=2026-10-19T14:23:04.7848049+00:00`. The only contemporaneous audit records S-3 Notes changing from `email inquiry` to empty at `2026-10-19T14:23:04.7841951+00:00`; it records no address change. Verified fact: the fingerprint was cleared without an address-column difference or address audit. Possible save-time geocode invalidation is interpretation. **Options:** retain Everyday's fingerprint, clear it to force later geocoding, or regenerate it only under separate authorization. **Question:** was any S-3 address/geocode reset intended, or should this unaudited clear be excluded?
- **S-233 soft deletion:** Everyday has active S-233, active links to C-210/C-211, and active C-210/C-211 with no archive token. Backup has S-233 inactive at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.0822800+00:00`; both links inactive; and both otherwise-unshared contacts inactive at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1775984+00:00`. One archive token ties those five rows. Each contact has zero other site links. Their four ContactMethod rows remain active even though the contacts are inactive, matching the application's soft-delete model. Activity 1362 at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1783814+00:00` records SiteRemoved for company C-76 but has null `SiteId`; details and the batch/timestamp sequence identify S-233, while direct foreign-key linkage is absent. **Options:** retain the active Everyday state, or—only if Rob confirms the deletion—reproduce the coordinated site/link/contact soft-delete with a mapped archive batch and newly linked history; do not copy the old Activity ID. **Questions:** should S-233 remain deleted, and should its two otherwise-unshared contacts remain restorable as part of the same batch?

### Facts, interpretations, limitations, and stop point

## Blue Ash contact transfer investigation findings (October 19, 2026)

**Status: completed after commit/push/remote-hash verification.** This was a read-only comparison and documentation task only. No recovery, repair, merge, database/source/schema/settings change, CRM execution, test, build, package, deployment, or email operation was performed.

### Safety and method

- Reused the prior disposable copies at `C:\Users\Dev\AppData\Local\Temp\crm-recovery-conflict-98c01711bfce4a07b22d74380ad2c617`; no new evidence copy was necessary and no `Z:` source file or original installation was opened or accessed during this assignment.
- Before SQLite access, the disposable main files matched the recorded source hashes: Backup `5A39A8E77372758DE417392A45B162BB94AD4DAC28721837578913F10E9C3EE6`, Everyday `308F7718F5FD8D3B4D5AA64B2AB02B5D3F374D8D3DE3787A2576397B90C54140`. Both 32,768-byte SHM copies matched `FD4C9FDA9CD3F9AE7C962B0DDF37232294D55580E1AA165AA06129B8549389EB`; both WAL copies were zero bytes at `E3B0C44298FC1C149AFBF4C8996FB92427AE41E4649B934CA495991B7852B855`.
- SQLite was pointed only at the disposable copies with `Mode=ReadOnly`, private cache, pooling disabled, and `PRAGMA query_only=ON`. Post-query file sizes and hashes remained identical. SQLite advanced the disposable SHM last-write timestamps without changing their hashes; main and WAL timestamps/hashes did not change. Because no source file was accessed, no source-side filesystem effect was possible in this assignment.
- Comparisons used sanitized IDs, booleans, timestamps, and truncated SHA-256 tokens of normalized identity/method values. No contact name, email, phone number, address, or raw customer row is published here.

### Confirmed findings

- Reliable site evidence identifies Blue Ash as active S-230 under C-76. Everyday S-230 has three active pre-existing contact links, C-205/C-206/C-207. Backup retains all three links unchanged as active and adds active links to C-508 and C-509; no existing Blue Ash contact was displaced.
- C-508 is a recreated duplicate of C-210, and C-509 is a recreated duplicate of C-211. Within each pair, normalized full name, JobTitle, Notes, Email value/normalization/validity, and OfficePhone value/normalization/validity match exactly. A whole-contact exact-name search found only each original and its corresponding new record. The new method rows use new IDs but reproduce both old method values exactly.
- The database represents the operation as recreation, not reassociation: Backup Activity 1358 records C-508 `ContactAdded` to S-230 at `2026-10-19T15:58:04.4570978+00:00`; Activity 1359 records C-509 `ContactAdded` at `2026-10-19T15:58:04.4695232+00:00`. There is no S-230 link for C-210 or C-211 in either snapshot and no ContactId-linked Activity row for those original IDs.
- The recreated records are not byte-for-byte state clones. C-210 has `IsInSalesforce=1` while C-508 has `0`; C-211 and C-509 both have `0`. All four recreated method rows have `IsPrimary=1`, whereas the matching original method rows have `0`. The new records also have October 19 creation timestamps rather than the originals' August 23 timestamps. C-509's S-230 link has `LastInteractionDate=2026-10-02`; the other old/new pair links have no LastInteractionDate.
- S-233 was deleted about 63 seconds after the two recreated contacts were added. S-233 became inactive at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.0822800+00:00`; C-210/C-211 became inactive at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1775984+00:00`; Activity 1362 records SiteRemoved at `2026-10-19T15:59:21.1783814+00:00`. One archive token marks S-233, its C-210/C-211 links, and the two original contacts. The active S-230 links and C-508/C-509 have no archive token.
- Therefore the deletion cascade did not deactivate the Blue Ash contacts or links. It deactivated C-210/C-211 because those original IDs still had only their S-233 links. Rob's active-use intent survived in Backup through C-508/C-509, but identity/history is split across duplicate IDs. Restoring S-233 as-is would revive C-210/C-211 and create two active duplicate pairs alongside C-508/C-509.

### Decision-ready recovery options — not authorized for execution

- **Recommended identity-preserving option:** treat C-508→C-210 and C-509→C-211 as recovery identity maps, retain original C-210/C-211 as the survivor records, add active S-230 links for those survivors, retain existing S-230 links C-205/C-206/C-207, and soft-delete only S-233 plus its S-233 links. Once the survivors have active S-230 links, do not archive/deactivate those contacts as “otherwise unshared.” A later S-233 restore would then reactivate only S-233 and its links to already-active contacts, avoiding duplicates and preserving the original identities/history.
- Under that option, preserve the originals' August creation history and C-210's `IsInSalesforce=1` unless Rob explicitly says otherwise. The recreated `IsPrimary=1` flags appear only on the new method rows and may be creation defaults; preserving the original method rows avoids inventing a preference change. Preserve the evidenced C-509/S-230 `LastInteractionDate=2026-10-02` on the mapped C-211/S-230 link if Rob confirms that date is meaningful.
- Preserve both October 19 ContactAdded audit records as recovered history with new Activity IDs and an explicit old-to-survivor map, but document that the operational action recreated existing people at a new site. Do not copy Backup Activity IDs, contact IDs, method IDs, or whole rows blindly. Any future recovery manifest must also preserve the previously documented October 16 Everyday events and all three existing Blue Ash contacts.
- **Literal-Backup option:** retain C-508/C-509 active at Blue Ash and archive C-210/C-211 with S-233. This most closely reproduces Backup, but it splits each person's identity/history and makes ordinary S-233 restoration produce active duplicates; it does not best satisfy Rob's full-history/restoration requirement.
- **Questions for Rob before any recovery authorization:** confirm C-210/C-211 should be the survivor identities rather than C-508/C-509; confirm C-210's existing Salesforce flag should remain; and confirm whether the October 2 LastInteractionDate on the C-509 Blue Ash link should be carried to the mapped C-211 link. No answer or recommendation authorizes implementation.

### Limitations and stop point

- Exact duplicate identity is strongly supported by Rob's stated intent plus exact name/title/two-method matches and the event sequence, but the database has no immutable person-identity key or explicit “transfer” event. The Activity model records the new rows as ContactAdded and provides no link-added event for the original IDs.
- This investigation did not inspect any area outside Blue Ash/S-233 and the four implicated contact IDs except for the sanitized whole-contact duplicate search needed to establish uniqueness. No recovery decision was applied. Stop here and await Rob's explicit authorization before any further investigation or recovery.

## Read-only dry-run failure investigation result — October 19, 2026

**Status: root cause confirmed from existing text source; no code or database access/execution.**

### Evidence inventory and limitation

- Existing harness source was available read-only at `C:\Users\Dev\.codex\visualizations\2026\10\20\01a15739-b7ba-70c1-be28-927112e34e5a\crm_recovery_dryrun.py` (30,391 bytes; SHA-256 `4EF3815C75347D6017A51EF12E562F1CB52812A6834D02E09B493AD6103E76F8`). The complete 655-line file was reviewed as text only and was not edited or executed.
- The reconciled public manifest, conflict decisions, timestamp provenance, and prior sanitized failure report are present in this handoff. No standalone execution log or stack traceback file exists in the harness directory. The prior captured output supplies only `IndexError: tuple index out of range`, timestamps, mappings, preflight data, clone path, and `rollback=PASS`; it contains no traceback frame or source line. Harness lines 627–641 explain that limitation: the exception handler serializes only `type(exc).__name__` and `str(exc)`, not a traceback. No SQLite file was opened, copied, inventoried, or searched to fill the gap.

### Confirmed root cause and exact operation

- The defect is in harness control logic, not the recovery manifest. `expected_rows()` lines 296–368 constructs keys through `key_for()` lines 145–146. A ContactMethod key is a one-element tuple `(ContactMethodId,)`; a SiteContact key is a two-element tuple `(SiteId, ContactId)`.
- Main lines 543–544 iterate the two new ContactMethod keys with loop variable `key`. With the deterministic map, the final value left in that variable is `(1059,)`, arity 1, with valid position `[0]` only.
- Main lines 545–546 then begin selecting new SiteContact rows with generator variable `k`. The intended candidate is `k=(SiteId, ContactId)`, arity 2, so the filter correctly reads `k[0]` for SiteId but incorrectly reads stale outer variable `key[1]` for ContactId. Because `key` is still `(1059,)`, position `[1]` does not exist and Python raises exactly `IndexError: tuple index out of range`. The tuple producer is `key_for()`; the faulty consumer is line 546. The smallest correction would replace both `key[1]` references on line 546 with `k[1]`. This is a proposal only; the harness was not changed.
- Source order establishes the last completed boundary more precisely than the earlier sanitized report: the C-507 Contact insert at line 542 and both ContactMethod inserts at lines 543–544 completed within the open transaction; evaluation failed in the SiteContact generator before any SiteContact insert at line 548. None of the profile/contact/link/archive updates, 16 Activity inserts, precommit checks, commit, or postcommit checks at lines 550–623 ran. All three completed inserts were inside the transaction that successfully rolled back, and the retained clone main hash matching E proves no durable recovery state. This does not make any partial manifest operation a pass.

### Impact assessment

- Deterministic mappings were not changed or contradicted: C-507→C-507, methods 1058/1059→1058/1059, C-508/C-509→C-210/C-211, and Activities 1347–1362→1351–1366 remain the reviewed plan. The exception resulted from using the wrong Python variable while filtering already constructed SiteContact keys; it did not arise from tuple contents supplied by the manifest.
- No Activity row was inserted, so none of the 16 historical Activity timestamps or relationship mappings—including the sole Activity 1362 `SiteId=S-233` repair—was exercised. Their preflight source evidence remains as previously documented, but result correctness is unverified.

It had the SQLite 3 header and no adjacent `-wal` or `-shm`; Rob confirmed the CRM was closed when copied.
- Analysis used only a byte-identical disposable copy at `C:\Users\Dev\AppData\Local\Temp\crm-priya-db-analysis-20261020\CRM.sqlite`, opened with SQLite URI `mode=ro&immutable=1` plus `PRAGMA query_only=ON`. Source and copy hashes matched. Final source re-hash was unchanged.

Relevant counts: Associate 20, CompanySalesAssignment 144, Site 426, Activity 1,373, Company 144, Contact 508, SiteContact 517.

### Schema, migration, and application finding

- The original normalized design combined two business roles in one `Associate` table. Import specification and `RcLogImporter` both resolve `Sales Person` and `Associate who made contact` through the same `GetOrCreateAssociate` routine. `Associate` has only `AssociateId`, case-insensitive unique `DisplayName`, and `IsActive`; it has no DT-associate/salesperson role discriminator.
- `CompanySalesAssignment.AssociateId` and `Activity.AssociateId` both reference that mixed table. Other Associate foreign keys are `AppUser.AssociateId`, `AgreementItemRate.DeletedByAssociateId`, and `AgreementDocument.DeletedByAssociateId`.
- Migration 7 added company salesperson/territory text. Migration 8 copied ownership to free-text `Site.SalesPerson`/`Site.TerritoryNumber`. Migration 18 added free-text `Site.DTAssociate` and backfilled it from the latest qualifying Activity's Associate. Migration 21 canonicalized Robert/Robet to Robert Nevins and linked AppUser to Associate. Migration 24 did not add role constraints.
- Therefore internal DeltaTrak associates and external salespeople were combined during normalization by design, and later denormalized into two unconstrained Site text fields. The requested rule “DT Associates should only be Robert Nevins, [Chelsie/Chelsea] Bonstein, Kala Pendergrass” cannot safely be enforced by deleting every other Associate row; those other rows carry salesperson assignments.
- Current site screens read/write free-text `Site.SalesPerson`; they do not use `CompanySalesAssignment` as their current-owner source. Site saves accept arbitrary salesperson spelling/case. Qualifying Activity insertions copy `Associate.DisplayName` into `Site.DTAssociate`. The current unapproved authentication branch also sets a site's DT associate from the signed-in AppUser Associate; because AppUser has no DT-role constraint, a future user linked to a salesperson row could recreate a mixed-role value. The legacy RC Log importer also accepts raw names and can recreate spelling variants if reused. These are recurrence risks, not authorization to change code.

### Group 1 — the three DT-associate identities

- Associate 2 `Robert Nevins`: already canonical. Related: 718 Activities, AppUser 1, one AgreementDocument deletion reference; no sales assignments.
- Associate 4 `Chelsie`: intended full name is the same person referred to by the request as Chelsea/Chelsea Bonstein, but the attachment explicitly spells the target `Chelsie Bonstein`. Related: 114 Activities; no sales assignments. Canonical spelling requires confirmation before any write.
- Associate 7 `Kala`: intended `Kala Pendergrass`. Related Activities 39 and 40, both at Site 17; no sales assignments.
- The other 17 Associate rows are not evidence of 17 additional DT associates. They include salespeople and operational identities and must not be blanket-deleted.

### Application recurrence risks and recommendations — no code changes made

- `Site.SalesPerson` and `Site.DTAssociate` are unconstrained text. Site save paths can reintroduce spelling/case variants. Later work should replace free text with canonical IDs or, at minimum, controlled role-filtered selectors and centralized alias normalization.
- `Associate` still mixes internal DT associates, outside salespeople, and operational identities with no role discriminator. Add an explicit role model or separate identity/assignment concepts before enforcing “three DT associates.”
- `RcLogImporter.GetOrCreateAssociate` creates both salespeople and activity associates from raw names. Future code should resolve approved aliases to canonical identities and reject or review unknown variants rather than insert them directly.
- Qualifying Activity creation copies the generic Associate display name into `Site.DTAssociate`. Enforce a DT-associate role at that boundary. The isolated, unapproved authentication branch also links AppUser to the same unrestricted Associate table; if that work is ever reconsidered, require the same role constraint.
- Current UI reads `Site.SalesPerson`, while normalized `CompanySalesAssignment` holds dated assignment history. Add one transactional handoff service that closes the old assignment, opens the replacement, and updates all in-scope Site rows together; prevent ordinary site edits from silently diverging from normalized current ownership.
- Preserve inactive alias/source identities or a durable correction-audit record when consolidating future duplicates so historical attribution remains explainable without editing Activity dates or source import rows.

## CRM approved-build and isolated display-test investigation — October 20, 2026

**Approved-build status: VERIFIED for the identified test source/package provenance; it is not a production release. Isolation status: FEASIBLE as a controlled proposal, but execution is BLOCKED pending separate authorization for writes to disposable assets.** No application was launched. No database was created, copied, or opened; no Z-drive artifact was accessed; no application repository file, branch, setting, schema, deployment artifact, or external system was changed.

### Approved source and package evidence

- Repository: `DarkPiglet/Weekly-Logger-Search-Application`. The handoff's explicit last approved source baseline is branch `codex/october13-crm-work` at full commit `3db33ca6ad96ecf72075dc2428069c2d8918ba0c`. Local and recorded `origin/codex/october13-crm-work` refs match that SHA, and GitHub returned the same commit. This source is unmerged but explicitly approved as the baseline.
- The newest demonstrably tested package on that baseline is `CRM-v10-test.20-october14-win-x64.zip`, built/tested at commit `1e5c36560ecc651b26a50b40639ebe01b9afa34f` on the same branch. `1e5c365` is an ancestor of `3db33ca`; their application trees are identical and differ only in `HANDOFF.md`.
- Local package: `C:\Users\Dev\source\repos\Weekly-Logger-Search-Application\artifacts\oct14-authoritative-build-20261014-000602\CRM-v10-test.20-october14-win-x64.zip`, 57,270,619 bytes, 316 entries, SHA-256 `73A705321CFA2858B52ABBD0409581361ADD0542EBF45393E2B6754DDE676CA0`. This matches the recorded October 14 hash. Read-only ZIP inspection confirmed `CRM.exe`, `Data/CRM.sqlite`, `Database/CRM_seed.sqlite`, and `Database/CRM_v24.sql`; no settings file, PDB, WAL, SHM, or journal is included. The recorded distributed path is `Z:\CRM-v10-test.20-october14-win-x64.zip`, but it was not accessed in this investigation.
- The package previously passed a schema-24 startup, integrity/foreign-key checks, a 302/302 regression, and responsive CRM/Monthly Drafts startup without data drift. That is evidence for a manual-test build, not production deployment approval.
- The unauthorized authentication implementation (`86a1dff` / branch `codex/application-authentication`) is not in the package ancestry. Test 22 descendants are awaiting review and their package is synthetic; neither is selected for this proposal.

### Static startup/shutdown findings

- Database resolution uses `CRM_DATABASE_PATH`, then legacy `WEEKLY_LOGGER_DATABASE_PATH`, then `Data\CRM.sqlite` under `AppContext.BaseDirectory`; the seed override follows the same current/legacy precedence (`Data/CRMPaths.vb:8-29`, `Data/CRMCompatibility.vb:14-18`). No command-line database override exists, and the process working directory does not control these defaults.
- `Form1_Load` calls `CRMDatabase.EnsureReady`, immediately runs the scheduled-backup check, constructs the repository, and calls `MigrateAgreementDocuments` before loading UI data (`Form1.vb:102-112`).
- `EnsureReady` creates the target from a legacy/seed database if the target is absent, creates an adjacent migration backup when schema is below 24, then opens the target read-write, applies migrations, and validates it (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:12-42,59-98,100-153`). Every normal connection is `ReadWrite`, enables foreign keys/busy timeout, and executes `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:44-56`). A schema-24 corrected copy avoids migrations/backups, but WAL/header/sidecar writes remain unavoidable.
- Settings resolve through `CRM_SETTINGS_PATH`, then legacy `WEEKLY_LOGGER_SETTINGS_PATH`, then application-adjacent `settings.json` (`OrderFormSettings.vb:26-31`). Loading can normalize paths and save the settings file, and the legacy/default agreement-folder migration can move managed documents and save settings (`OrderFormSettings.vb:34-53,120-152`). A missing settings file defaults agreement documents to live Local AppData.
- Startup `MigrateAgreementDocuments` opens a read-write transaction, prepares/renames company folders and ownership markers, moves files when present, and can update `AgreementDocument.StoredRelativePath` (`Data/CRMRepository.vb:88-198`; `AgreementDocumentStorage.vb:10-55`). It cannot be treated as display-only I/O. Its root must be an isolated local folder and any writes must be authorized only there and in the disposable DB.
- Scheduled backup runs once at startup and then every 24 hours; an empty `BackupFolder` disables creation (`Form1.vb:90,106-110,993-1016`; `Data/CRMBackupService.vb:13-43`). Diagnostic logs default to live Local AppData but support `CRM_LOG_FOLDER`/legacy override and write only on handled/unhandled errors (`DiagnosticLog.vb:11-45`).
- This package has no startup authentication. Graph, browsers, Excel, exports, geocoding, and Monthly Drafts are user-invoked paths, not main-window startup work. Network access should nevertheless be disabled and those commands must remain untouched.
- Normal close stops/disposes the backup timer. If the UI becomes dirty, close offers Save/Discard/Cancel and Save invokes database writes (`Form1.vb:1013-1016,2061-2090`). `SaveMySettingsOnExit=True` is configured, but this commit has no `Settings.settings`; whether the VB runtime creates profile metadata is not proven. A disposable Windows profile/sandbox is therefore required.

### Startup-write risk table

| Path/asset | Trigger and possible write | Required mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| Disposable `CRM.sqlite` | `EnsureReady` and every repository connection open read-write and set WAL; migration if below v24 | Use only a fresh local working copy; immutable preflight must prove v24; retain a sealed reference copy; allow DB/WAL/SHM writes only in the disposable root |
| Adjacent migration backup | Target below v24 | Fail closed unless both `user_version` and `SchemaVersion` are 24; reject any created backup as a preflight failure |
| Application `Data\CRM.sqlite` / seed | Used if the override is missing or target disappears | Set current and legacy DB/source variables; remove/replace the extracted default DB with a fail-closed sentinel and deny writes to extracted `Data`; verify target exists immediately before launch |
| `settings.json` | Load-time normalization/migration may save | Precreate an isolated absolute settings file; set current and legacy settings variables; hash it before/after |
| Agreement documents | Startup may create/rename folders/markers, move files, and update DB paths | Point settings to an empty isolated root, preflight DB paths/markers, authorize any resulting writes only there; never point to live Local AppData/network/USB paths |
| Backup folder | Immediate weekly check can create/prune backups | Set `BackupFolder` to blank; verify no inherited settings value |
| Diagnostic logs | Startup exception/unhandled error | Set current and legacy log variables to an isolated log folder |
| User profile/config | Possible VB exit metadata; Local AppData defaults if isolation fails | Use a fresh disposable Windows Sandbox/VM or local test profile with no mapped Z drive and no production profile access |
| Network/Graph/browser | Not automatic in this build, but available through commands | Disable network; do not open Monthly Drafts, shipping, Settings, Maintenance, export, or email paths |

### Proposed isolated procedure — not executed

1. Obtain explicit authorization for one launch and for startup writes only to named disposable paths. Reverify the selected package hash and record pre-operation hashes for `Z:\CRM.sqlite`, `Z:\CRM_corrected.sqlite`, and `Z:\CRM_correction_manifest.json` without opening either database for write.
2. Create a new non-reparse local test root. Extract the verified October 14 package there. Copy `Z:\CRM_corrected.sqlite` twice: one sealed read-only reference and one working database. Hash both against the recorded corrected hash.
3. Using immutable read-only SQL, fail unless the working/reference files are standalone schema 24, integrity is `ok`, foreign-key check is empty, and the approved manifest still matches. Do not migrate.
4. Create isolated settings, logs, documents, outputs, and profile folders under the test root. Settings must use absolute local paths, blank `BackupFolder`, and no Z/network/USB/current-profile path. Pre-stage only safe agreement-folder markers derived from read-only DB metadata; do not copy production documents.
5. Set both current and legacy environment overrides to the isolated assets. Make the extracted default `Data` path fail closed. Validate every resolved path and ancestor, reject reparse points/network drives/Z paths, confirm Z is not mapped or accessible inside the test profile, and disable network.
6. Launch only the verified package's `CRM.exe`. Do not open Settings, Maintenance, Monthly Drafts, export, shipping, order/email, or edit/save paths. Capture display evidence, then close with no dirty state; if an unexpected save prompt appears, do not select Save.
7. Rehash all three Z-drive artifacts and require exact pre/post equality. Validate the disposable DB and sidecars, compare logical schema/content to the sealed reference and manifest, and separately report expected physical hash changes caused by WAL. Rollback is discard-and-recopy only; nothing is copied back. Retain the isolated evidence until Rob reviews it, then delete the disposable root under separate confirmation.

The UI has no canonical Associate-management view, hides the DT Associate grid column (`Form1.Designer.vb:778-782`), does not display ActivityId, and free text cannot prove inactive aliases are unavailable for entry.
- Correct spellings remain `Chelsie Bonstein`, `Rachelle Pombo`, and `Anakaren Reyes`; no variant is authorization to edit.

### Isolation blocker and stopping point

### Required decision

## Existing CRM virtual-machine isolation investigation — October 20, 2026

**Classification: SUITABLE ONLY AFTER SPECIFICALLY IDENTIFIED AND APPROVED CHANGES.** The current Windows instance is already a guest VM; absence of nested Windows Sandbox or Hyper-V tooling inside it is not evidence against using this VM. It is unsuitable *as currently configured* because the guest has direct read/write visibility to the host-provided `Z:` share, can read the preserved CRM production artifacts and guest documents, and has an active routed network interface. No CRM process was launched, no files were copied, and no VM, drive, network, permission, database, code, or application setting was changed.

### Verified guest and virtualization evidence

- Guest firmware/registry identify `SystemManufacturer=QEMU`, product `Ubuntu 24.04 PC (Q35 + ICH9, 2009)`, and an Ubuntu EDK II firmware build. The QEMU Guest Agent, SPICE Agent, VirtIO-FS service, WinFsp launcher, and corresponding VirtIO drivers are installed; QEMU Guest Agent, SPICE Agent, VirtIO-FS, and WinFsp were running. The environment is therefore a QEMU/KVM-style Windows guest, likely managed from an Ubuntu/libvirt host, although the physical host and its exact management configuration cannot be inspected or proven from inside the guest.
- The guest has one QEMU virtual hard disk (`C:`), two QEMU virtual optical devices (`D:` and `E:`), and a host share mounted as `Z:`. `D:` contains a read-only `OFFICE14` UDF medium; `E:` is empty/not ready. No other filesystem drive, SMB mapping, persistent mapped network drive, or `SUBST` drive was observed.
- `Z:` is not SMB. It is labelled `VM-Share`; the active VirtIO-FS service explicitly provides access to host-shared directories. Windows reports the mount as read/write NTFS, and its root ACL includes `Everyone: FullControl`. Write access was not exercised.

### Resources currently accessible from the guest

- The guest can enumerate `Z:` and read `Z:\CRM.sqlite`, `Z:\CRM_corrected.sqlite`, `Z:\CRM_correction_manifest.json`, `Z:\Data for Priya.docx`, CRM packages, test documents, and other host-shared content. This directly proves current access to production databases/documents. The known database files were opened only for a one-byte read test and were not opened read-write or changed.
- No SMB connection or ordinary mapped network drive is active, but this does not restrict VirtIO-FS or prove that unmounted network resources are unreachable.
- The active guest Ethernet interface has DHCP address `192.168.122.24/24`, gateway/DNS `192.168.122.1`, and a default route. Windows Firewall is on but permits outbound traffic. DNS resolution and the authorized GitHub fetch/push path work; a direct TCP probe from the restricted Codex process did not, showing that network access depends on execution context rather than being reliably absent. The libvirt-style subnet suggests NAT, but NAT versus bridge and access to host/LAN services cannot be proven from the guest alone.
- The only connected USB-class devices observed were the virtual xHCI controller and root hub; no USB peripheral or USB storage device was present. The controller means later host-side passthrough/hot-plug remains possible unless the VM configuration prevents it. No such prevention could be verified from the guest.
- The active interactive console belongs to guest user `Dev`; investigation commands run as restricted user `CodexSandboxOffline`. That restricted identity nevertheless has explicit read/execute access to `C:\Users\Dev\Documents` and `C:\Users\Dev\source`, while `Dev` has full control. Thus neither likely launch identity is presently isolated from the live guest profile and repository/documents. An active SPICE Agent is another host-integration channel; no SPICE WebDAV filesystem mount was observed, but host-side clipboard/file-transfer policy is unverified.
- Final preservation recheck: `Z:\CRM.sqlite` remained `FF4EC6C909C38171F5C45258A7147CA4EB79787F8F211D4C0B02C26A97DD7A5D`; `Z:\CRM_corrected.sqlite` remained `B4B5D575FA9E494605F3BEBEBA9D9B5F85B82867453ED5D6816B03148BCE5B13`; and `Z:\CRM_correction_manifest.json` remained `8AA91C24DD1F70CF6DC737A94508BB811DDECDFAC8B2EEA942B9BFB6D3992E24`. Their recorded timestamps were unchanged, and no CRM process was running.

### Required controls and uncertainties

- The reliable control for `Z:` is a host-hypervisor change: power off the guest and detach the `VM-Share` VirtIO-FS device (or use a clone without it), then boot and verify that `Z:` and all known production paths are absent. Stopping the guest service or relying on path environment variables is weaker and does not prove the host share cannot return.
- Disconnect the VM's virtual NIC at the hypervisor, or attach it to a proven no-uplink isolated network, for the display run. Guest firewall defaults are insufficient because outbound is currently allowed. This must not alter the physical host's connectivity.
- Confirm in the host VM definition that no host disk/folder other than an explicitly scoped disposable transfer mechanism is attached, USB passthrough and hot-plug are absent, and SPICE file-transfer/shared-folder features are disabled. Guest-side inventory cannot prove those host policies.
- Run the future test from a fresh non-administrator guest account/profile that cannot read `C:\Users\Dev`, with all database/settings/log/document/output paths under a disposable local root. The present Codex sandbox identity is not sufficient because it can read the live Dev profile. Creating the account or changing ACLs is not authorized now.
- After any approved changes, reverify from inside the guest: no `Z:`/SMB/host share, no default route or external DNS, no USB storage, no readable Dev/production paths, and only the intended disposable assets. Retain the sealed reference and working database rules from the pending preparation task. CRM preparation and launch remain separately gated.

### Simplest reliable next step

Prefer a disposable snapshot/clone of this existing QEMU VM so Rob's Excel-testing VM remains unchanged. Before booting the test clone, configure it with no VirtIO-FS share, no network adapter/uplink, no USB passthrough, no SPICE file transfer, and a test-only non-admin profile; expose only a narrowly scoped disposable local test disk/root. If a clone is impractical, the same device removals can be applied temporarily to the powered-off existing VM after a recoverable snapshot. Either approach requires Rob's explicit approval for host-side VM configuration, snapshot/clone creation, guest-profile/ACL setup, and later restoration. It does not authorize copying CRM artifacts, preparing databases/settings, or launching CRM; those remain blocked pending a new instruction after isolation is independently verified.


## October 14 CRM startup database-path investigation — October 20, 2026

**Result: target-path and fallback logic VERIFIED by source inspection; runtime failure details remain untested.** Reviewed the approved October 14 package source at commit `1e5c36560ecc651b26a50b40639ebe01b9afa34f`, whose application files match approved baseline `3db33ca6ad96ecf72075dc2428069c2d8918ba0c`. No executable was launched, no database or package was opened for write, and no preparation, VM/network change, deployment, or application/repository change occurred other than this handoff report.

### Exact startup path resolution

- `Form1.New` loads settings first; `Form1_Load` then calls `CRMDatabase.EnsureReady()` with no path arguments (`Form1.vb:74-106`). There is no startup command-line database override and no second startup call that selects another target.
- Target database precedence is: (1) nonblank `CRM_DATABASE_PATH`; (2) if current is absent/blank, nonblank legacy `WEEKLY_LOGGER_DATABASE_PATH`; (3) otherwise `AppContext.BaseDirectory\Data\CRM.sqlite` (`Data/CRMCompatibility.vb:14-18`; `Data/CRMPaths.vb:8-20`). `Path.GetFullPath` normalizes an override; a relative override therefore depends on the process current directory, not the application directory. Blank/whitespace current and legacy values fall through. The October 14 ZIP contains `Data\CRM.sqlite`, but the code—not ZIP presence—controls selection.
- `EnsureReady` resolves both the selected target and source paths before checking target existence (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:12-23`). Source precedence is: (1) nonblank `CRM_SOURCE_DATABASE_PATH`; (2) legacy `WEEKLY_LOGGER_SOURCE_DATABASE_PATH`; (3) `AppContext.BaseDirectory\Database\CRM_seed.sqlite` if that file exists; (4) otherwise `AppContext.BaseDirectory\Database\WeeklyLogger_seed.sqlite`, whether or not the legacy seed exists (`Data/CRMPaths.vb:22-29`). An invalid source override can therefore fail path normalization even when the target already exists; a merely missing source file is irrelevant until target initialization is needed.
- If the selected target exists, it is used in place. The source and bundled/default target are not considered as substitutes. Startup next checks/makes a migration backup when needed, opens this same target read-write, enables foreign keys/busy timeout, sets WAL mode, validates schema, migrates through v24, and checks integrity/foreign keys (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:33-55,100-175`).
- If the selected target does not exist and its filename equals `CRM.sqlite` (case-insensitive), CRM first checks for `WeeklyLogger.sqlite` in the *same selected directory*. If present, it creates an independent SQLite backup copy at the selected target. This applies to the default directory and to an overridden path named `CRM.sqlite` (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:23-30`).
- Otherwise the chosen source path above is copied into the selected target. Copying uses a read-only source connection, a new `<target>.initializing` database, SQLite `BackupDatabase`, integrity/foreign-key validation, then `File.Move` to the target; any pre-existing `.initializing` file is deleted and a failed temporary copy is cleaned up (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:59-98`). This initializes the selected target; it does not continue using the legacy/seed file.
- If neither qualifying adjacent legacy database nor chosen source is available, CRM throws `FileNotFoundException`; it does not create an empty database and does not fall back to the packaged `Data\CRM.sqlite`. If `File.Exists` reports the modern seed present, it wins over the legacy seed; if that chosen modern seed is invalid or later proves inaccessible, there is no retry with the legacy seed. If `File.Exists` reports it absent (including some access-error cases), the legacy seed path is selected instead.
- Migration scripts have no alternative search chain: startup begins with `AppContext.BaseDirectory\Database\CRM_v5.sql`, then resolves `CRM_v5.sql` through `CRM_v24.sql` from that directory. A missing or incorrectly version-stamping script throws; CRM does not select another database (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:19-21,112-134`).

### Missing, inaccessible, or invalid target behavior

- **Missing:** CRM attempts the adjacent-legacy rule, then source-copy rule, at the already selected target path. It may create the target directory, delete a stale `.initializing` file, and create/move the new database. Failure to find/open/copy the source or create/move the target propagates as an exception; no other target is tried.
- **Inaccessible/locked/read-only:** there is no access-error fallback. If `File.Exists` reports the target present, read-only version inspection and then a read-write/WAL connection are attempted; any access/lock/write failure propagates. If Windows/.NET reports an inaccessible path as nonexistent, code follows the missing-target initialization branch and normally fails when reading the source or creating the target. The exact `File.Exists` result, SQLite error, timeout, and sidecar behavior for a particular ACL/share/lock require runtime testing.
- **Invalid content/schema:** a non-SQLite or corrupt file fails during read-only `PRAGMA user_version`, backup validation, opening, or integrity checks. A valid SQLite database below schema 4 is rejected; a database above v24 is rejected; integrity or foreign-key failures are rejected. None causes target switching. A below-v24 file can receive a timestamped adjacent migration backup before later validation fails; a newer or v24-invalid file is opened read-write and WAL mode is requested before the rejection (`Data/CRMDatabase.vb:33-39,100-110,136-175`).
- All exceptions escaping `EnsureReady` are caught by `Form1_Load`: CRM writes a diagnostic entry if possible, displays `CRM could not finish starting: <message>`, and calls `Close()` (`Form1.vb:102-128`). Source therefore proves fail-and-close rather than silent target switching. Whether the packaged UI closes cleanly in every failure mode, the exact message/provider error, and which WAL/SHM/backup/temp artifacts a particular failure leaves require controlled runtime tests and were not tested.

### Silent behavior versus errors

- Silent path selection occurs only when a higher-precedence environment value is absent/blank, when the modern seed is absent and the legacy seed path is selected, or when a missing target is initialized from the adjacent legacy/source database. These branches do not prompt the user.
- Once a nonblank target override is selected, missing/inaccessible/invalid conditions never cause CRM to switch to the app-adjacent `Data\CRM.sqlite` or another target. Initialization copies data *to the configured target*; subsequent work uses that target. Failures error out and close startup.

### Startup writes unrelated to choosing the target

- Before database resolution, `OrderFormSettings.Load` reads `CRM_SETTINGS_PATH`, then legacy `WEEKLY_LOGGER_SETTINGS_PATH`, then app-adjacent `settings.json`. Path normalization can rewrite that settings file. Legacy agreement-folder migration can create the new folder, copy/delete managed documents, and save settings. Settings read/JSON errors are silently replaced with defaults, but failures from the later agreement-folder migration can block startup before `EnsureReady` (`OrderFormSettings.vb:26-53,120-152`; `AgreementDocumentStorage.vb:65-110`).
- A startup exception writes/appends a daily log under nonblank `CRM_LOG_FOLDER`, then legacy `WEEKLY_LOGGER_LOG_FOLDER`, or `%LocalAppData%\DeltaTrak\CRM\Logs`; it creates the directory and deletes logs beyond the newest 30 (`DiagnosticLog.vb:11-45`). Logging failure is swallowed.
- After database readiness, the weekly-backup check runs immediately. Blank `BackupFolder` performs no backup; otherwise a backup older than seven days triggers directory creation, a temporary SQLite backup, final move/timestamp, and pruning beyond ten backups. Backup errors are logged and shown as a warning rather than selecting another database (`Form1.vb:106-110,993-1010`; `Data/CRMBackupService.vb:13-42,84-107`).
- Startup then calls `CRMRepository.MigrateAgreementDocuments`, which opens the selected database through the same read-write/WAL connection, begins a transaction, and may create/rename/delete agreement folders/owner markers, move documents, update `AgreementDocument.StoredRelativePath`, and clean empty folders (`Data/CRMRepository.vb:19-30,88-213`; `AgreementDocumentStorage.vb:10-55`). Subsequent initial data loads also use read-write/WAL connections even for queries. No automatic network, browser, email, export, or deployment action was found on the main-form startup path.

### Runtime-only evidence still required

No launch was authorized. A later isolated test would be needed to prove the effective current directory for relative overrides; inherited environment values in the packaged process; concrete ACL/read-only-share/locked-file behavior; exact SQLite exceptions and sidecars; failure-dialog/close behavior; and whether the actual settings, backup age, or agreement records trigger their conditional writes. Existing source/tests establish copying and migration intent but do not cover the complete missing/inaccessible/corrupt startup matrix. CRM preparation and execution remain blocked pending separate explicit authorization.
